6. दू० अ० कन्दकनिर्तनो नाम षष्ठः स० । 40b.

154------------------------------------------------

1. 7. इ० अ० तपोवनप्रवेशो न० सप्तमः स० । 46.
2. 8. इ० अ० अन्तःपुरविलापो नामाष्टमः । 55a-b.
3. 9. इ० अ० कुमारान्विषणी नाम नवमः स० । 62.
4. 10. इ० अ० अश्वघोषकृत अश्वभिगमनो नाम दशमः स० । 66.
5. 11. इ० अ० कामविगर्हणी नामैकादशः स० । 73.
6. 12. इ० अ० अराडदर्शनो नाम द्वादशः स० । 81b.
7. 13. इ० अ० अ० मारविनयो नाम त्रयोदशः । 88.
8. 14. इ० अ० अभिसंवोधनसंस्तवनो नाम चतुर्दशः । 93b.
9. 15. इ० अ० धर्मचक्रपरिवर्तनाध्येषणं नाम पञ्चदशः स० । 100b.
10. 16. इ० अ० धर्मचक्रपरिवर्तनं नाम षोडशः स० । 108.
11. 17. इ० अ० ऽलंविनीयाचिकं नाम सप्तदशः स० । 114b.

The MS. terminated originally at leaf 109: on the back of which we get a long colophon, chiefly in the vernacular, of which the following is the first part (intended for *ḍlokas*):

भृगुन्याणांकयुग्मर्षे मर्गि मासे ऽसिते स्मरे ।

अमृतानन्देन लिखितं बुद्धकाव्यं सुदुर्लभं ॥ (sic, contra *metrum*)

सर्वत्रान्विष्य नो लब्धा चतुःसर्गं च निर्मितं ।

चतुर्दशं पंचदशं षोडशं सप्तदशं तथा ॥ २ ॥

Then follow some lines, chiefly in vernacular, as to the reigning monarch (Rājendra Vikrama). The remainder of the MS. (109\*—114), written in the same hand, on leaves fresher looking and slightly smaller, contains ch. 17.

#### Add. 1389.

Paper; 61 leaves, 7 lines, 10 × 4 in.; xvii<sup>th</sup> or early xviii<sup>th</sup> cent.

GOPĪ-CANDRA-NĀṬAKA (vernacular).

Siddhinrisimha is mentioned at 61a l. 7.

Compare Dr Pischel's Catal. der Deutschen Morg. Gesellsch. No. 4 (also a vernacular play), p. 6 med.

155------------------------------------------------

At 1b, l. 1, Govinda-candra, 'Bangero adhipati', is mentioned.  
Begins :

श्रीं नमो नृत्यनाथाय ।

... अथ गोपीचन्द्रनाटक लिख्यते ।

Ends :

इति गोपीचन्द्रप्रथमाङ्कः समाप्तः । 18b.

The latter part of the MS. is a good deal damaged.

### Add. 1395.

Palm-leaf; 123 leaves, 5 lines,  $13\frac{1}{2} \times 2$  in.; dated N. s. 505 (A.D. 1385).

### PAṄCA-RAKSHĀ.

For chapters see Add. 1325 ch. 1, 31a, also the verse ये धर्मा° at the end.

At the end of the book after ये धर्मा° we find :

श्रेयो ऽस्तु । सप्तत् ५०५ कार्तिक शुक्ल अष्टम्यां तित्यौ स-  
निम्बरवासरे । श्रीश्री जयस्थिति राजमल्लदेवस्य विजयराज्ये  
लिखितं ।

Another hand on the cover, newer-looking but in the same style of character, gives a date (? of recitation) N. s. 572, month Jyeshṭha, in a vernacular.

### Add. 1398.

Paper; 46 leaves, 7 lines,  $13 \times 4\frac{1}{2}$  in.; modern.

### MAṄICUḌĀVADĀNA.

Begins and ends as Add. 874, which see; compare also Add. 1375. The text however seems independent of both these MSS.

### Add. 1400.

Paper; 18 leaves, 6 lines,  $12 \times 3$  in.; dated N. s. 888 (A.D. 1768).

### VASUDHĀRANĪ-KATHĀ (?) or SUCANDRĀVADĀ[NA].

The second title is written on the left-hand margin of each verso.

156------------------------------------------------

The above date though palæographically possible is not altogether trustworthy, as it is added in a somewhat later hand.

The work is a tale of a rich merchant Sucandra who, with his wife Candravatī, is reduced to poverty, and on consulting the Buddha is told of the magic efficacy of the Vasudhārā-dhāraṇī (10a). Compare Add. 1355.

Begins with the same words as Add. 1357 (which compare, and R. A. S. Cat. ib. cit.); after which :

अस्ति तस्मिं समये भगवान् जम्बद्विपस्य काश्यायां महा-  
नगर्थास्विहरति स्म ।

Ends :

इति सुचन्द्रो गृहपतिः प्रच्छति श्रीवसुधारणीकथा भगवान्  
श्राक्यमुनितथागतो भाषितः समाप्तः ॥ ये धर्मा हेतु etc.

लिखितेयं सुवर्णप्राणिमहानगरे रत्नकेतुमहाविहारवस्थित  
(sic) श्रीश्राक्यमुनिन लिपापितामिति (sic) ॥

#### Add. 1401.

Paper; 17 leaves, 8—9 lines, 4 × 9 in.; dated N. S. 926  
(A.D. 1806).

VASUNDHARĀ-VRATA (from the Vratāvadānamālā).

The MS. has been recently pieced and mended in most leaves.

Begins :

अशोको ऽथोपगृहं तं व्यजिन्नपल्लताञ्जलिः ।

Upagupta then tells the story of a king Sūryodaya who gained offspring, wealth and power by the observance of the Vasundharā-vow. After 32 verses, the tale proceeds in prose :

ततः केचित्साश्वर्या जना राजानं विज्ञाय...

Ends :

इति श्रीव्रतावदानमालायां श्रीवसुन्धराव्रतकथनं समाप्तं ।

Then the date, as above.

Add. 1405.

Coarse paper; 55 leaves, 5 lines,  $9\frac{1}{2} \times 2\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. s. 734 (A.D. 1614).

CAITYA-PUṄGALA.

Compare R. A. S. Cat. No. 22.

A very faulty copy. The work is a sūtra on the ritual connected with caityas, the hearer being a king, Indrapriṣṭha.

Begins :

श्रीं नमो बुद्धाय ॥

नमस्ते हेमगर्भाय नमस्ते भक्तिवत्सल ।

जिनधातु नमसुभ्यं नमस्ते वाग्मया[य च] ॥

इन्द्रपृष्ठ उवाच । आयुभ्रान् वसुवन्धे तथा चैत्यं प्रवक्ष्यामि ।

पृथिव्यां निष्ठभूतानि स्थावराणि चलानि च ॥

वसुवन्धोवाच । भो वसुधाधिपेन्द्रपृष्ठ शृणु । ° ॥

एवमया अतमेकस्मिन्समये भगवान् गन्धर्वत्यां महानगर्था वि-  
हरति स्म ।

अनेकदेवनागयचगन्धर्वासुरगुरुडकिन्नरमहीरगादि...

The following sections are marked :

इति चैत्यपुङ्गरभट्टारके स्थापनात्फलं ॥ 6b.

इति चैत्यपुङ्गलभट्टारके अर्चणात्फलं ॥ 7b.

इति चैत्यपुंगरभट्टारक अर्चनात्फलं । 16.

इत्यहीरात्रव्रतकथा राजनेन्द्रपृष्ठे (*sic*) । 24.

इति कार्त्तिकव्रत अर्चनकथाराजनेन्द्रपिष्ठ (*sic*) । 26 (*a-b*).

Ends :

इति चैत्यपुङ्गर आर्यसंधभट्टारकार्चण(*sic*)वर्तनकथा प्रोक्तं  
सूत्रान्तपरिच्छेद समाप्तः । सं ७३४...etc. (see above).

लिखित श्रीवज्राचार्यसूर्यदे[वेन]

(The full name of the scribe and some 'scribe's verses' are added in a much smaller hand.)

158------------------------------------------------

Add. 1409.

Palm-leaf; 143 leaves, 5 lines, 13 × 2 in.; dated N. s. 480 (A.D. 1360).

RĀMĀṆKA-NĀṢIKĀ by DHARMAGUPTA, and fragment.

Besides the leaves numbered as above there is a cover and a separate leaf with a summary (?) of each act in a vernacular. 43 is missing.

The MS. is the author's autograph (see below).

The work is a play in Sanskrit and the usual Prākṛit by Dharmagupta, also called "Bālavāgīṭvara" (? a mere complimentary title), son of Rāmadāsa, a magistrate of Nepal (see below), in four acts.

The plot is taken from the myth of Rāvaṇa and seems to have no Buddhist references; the Nāṇḍi, too, ends:

पातु युष्मान्मुरारिः

The Prastāvanā begins thus (after the Nāṇḍi):

ततो नान्यन्ते विनायकः सपरिवारः।

विना। देवि सिद्धिदायिनि। तातः श्रीचिपुरान्तकी विजयते नाद्यावतारेश्वरः प्रत्यूहप्रशमाय तत्र जगतां सो ऽहं प्रवृत्ती भुवि॥

Further on (2a, ll. 3, 4) the original place of production is thus referred to:

मणिनागशिरोमणिदीधितिभी रुचिरं सुकृतादृतथा चयति  
[? leg. चायते] ललितापुरमेतदिहादरतो गणनाथ विनाशय  
विघ्नगणं।

On 2b the Vināyaka enquires:

कस्य कृतिरियं।

To which the answer is:

अस्ति विद्यार्णवो बालवागीश्वरः कविः। तेन श्रीधर्मगुप्तेन  
कृता रामाङ्कनाटिका

Another speaker thus continues:

किमुच्यत एवं। अत्र हि॥

चातुन्नातस्य सत्यम्भुवि नृपतिपदनं त्यक्त्वान् राघवो ऽसौ

159------------------------------------------------

दभात् सौमित्रिगुप्तामहृत विपिनतो रावणस् तस्य पत्नीं।  
 मित्रीकृत्यानुजौ तौ कपि +निच्छति+ ययोः सेतुनाश्चिखिलङ्ग  
 ह्वा लङ्गाधिनाथान् निजपुरिसकलः प्राप्नु--७--॥ (a few  
 letters obliterated). King Dācaratha is then introduced with his  
 followers, and the first act begins.

Act 1 ends: इति श्रीरामाङ्गनाटिकायां प्रथमो ऽङ्कः। 40b.

Act 2 ,, इ० अ० द्वितीयो ऽङ्कः। 70b.

Act 3 ,, इ० अ० तृतीय०। 102b.

Act 4 ,, इ० अ० चतुर्थ०। 160b.

After this we read:

समाप्ता श्रीरामाङ्गनाटिका॥ विख्यातो जगतीतले स जयति  
 श्रीकण्ठपूजापरो नेपालावनपालमण्डलगुरुः श्रीरामदासः।

Then a verse in praise of the poet's education, etc.:

पायम्यायमतीतवाक्य...

Then the subscription, giving the date:

श्रीधर्मगुप्तः कृती। पित्रा पुत्रकृपापरेण निपुणं शास्त्रान्वयं  
 शिचित् एताम्भावरसीञ्चलां स कृतवान् रामाङ्गितान्नाटिकां।  
 श्रेयो ऽस्तु। सम्वत् ४८० शुक्लिकदशम्यां रवि वासो। तेनैव धर्म-  
 गुप्तेन श्रीमता रामदासिना। बालवागीश्वरेणैयं लिखिता रा-  
 माङ्गनाटिका॥ शुभमस्तु सर्वदा॥

Leaves 141—2 are *namaskāras*, or short prayers, to various beings connected with the stage.

Another leaf, in a different hand, is a "namaskaratāvali" for the various acts.

There are also two leaves, unnumbered, apparently from a play on a similar subject, *Sitā* and other characters being named.

#### Add. 1411.

Paper; 381 leaves, 7 lines, 17 × 4 in.; dated N. s. 952  
 (A.D. 1832).

BHADRAKALPĀVADĀNA.

Copies exist at Calcutta and Paris.

160------------------------------------------------

The work is supposed to be a dialogue between Açoka and Upagupta, and consists of 28 adhyāyas.

Begins after invocation, नवा श्रीमत् चिरं... , thus :

वक्ष्ये श्रीशाक्यराजस्वपुरगतकथासुन्दरं मोचहेतुं चातुर्वर्ग-  
भिकांशाः शृणुत बुद्धजना भद्रकल्याणदानं ॥

Jinaçrī is first introduced, reminding Jayaçrī (cf. Burn. *Intr.* 221) that he has heard from him the “Çrilalita-vyūha,” and desiring now to hear “Çākyendra-pratyāgamana-satkathā.” Jayaçrī complies, and accordingly Açoka is introduced as enquiring of Upagupta as follows:

जातकावलिसौन्दर्यं विस्तराख्याकथामृतं ।  
पीतं भवन्मुखाम्भोजोद्गतं पापचर्यकरं ॥  
पिपासुरधुना बुद्धस्वपुरागतसत्तुधां ।  
कपिलाख्यपुरे किं किमभद्रं विरहसङ्कुले ॥  
यशोधरापन्नसत्त्वा कीदृशी विरहार्दिता ।  
गौतमी चतुराशीतिसहस्रिकाश्च तत्प्रियाः ॥  
शुद्धीधनादयः सर्वे कथं विस्मरिता प्रभुं ।  
भिक्षवश्च कृतास्तेन कथं लोका प्रपालिता ॥  
कियद्वर्षात्यये चाभूत् पितापुत्रसमागमः ।  
तत्कथामृतदानेन शोकानलप्रशान्तये ॥

These verses give a kind of short summary of the contents of the book (compare also the titles of the chapters, below). As the work is entirely in verse, we are not surprised at the confession of posteriority to the Jātaka literature and to the (Lalita-)Vistara. For the connexion with the former, see, for example, ch. 34; of the latter the work may be regarded as in some sense a poetical continuation, as it treats of the middle and later life of the Buddha.

The work also has some interest as bearing a certain analogy of plan and subject to the Pāli Vinaya-Pitakam. Thus the first chapter corresponds to Mahāvagga I. 6, describing the beginning of the Buddha's ministry, and his meeting with the Pañcavargika Bhikshus. After a number of chapters devoted to Gopā and Yaçodharā, the conversion of several of the celebrated disciples is described, in somewhat different order to that of the Pāli, though chs. 20, 21 correspond to Mahāvagga I. 22—24. In some episodes however the

161------------------------------------------------

legends give many particulars not contained in other accounts hitherto noticed. Thus in ch. 35, Rāhula, before his conversion, is induced by Āuddhodana to marry: he accordingly marries Kamalā, and has a son Sakalānanda (see next chapter). Rāhula is himself received (as in the Southern account) by Maudgalyāyana and Āriputra.

The titles of the chapters are:

1. 1. इति श्रीभद्रकल्यावदाने ब्रह्मादिस्तुतिष्ठतं खपुरप्रत्यागमप्रस्थानं नाम प्रथमी सध्यायः । 11b.
2. 2. इ° श्री° यशोधरागर्भसंधारणी नाम द्वितीया सध्यायः । 20b.
3. 3. इ° अ° अश्रीकीपगुप्तसंभाषणे देवदत्तकामलीभान्मत्तभूतो न° हतः । 30b.
4. 4. इ° अ° अ° गोपातीर्थप्रवर्तनो न° चतुर्थः । 48b-49.
5. 5. इ° अ° गोपाप्रत्यागमनो न° पञ्चमः । 55.
6. 6. इ° अ° गोपाग्निपातनं न° षष्ठः । 62b.
7. 7. इ° अ° गोपास्रुगुपातनं न° सप्तः । 69.
8. 8. इ° अ° यशोधरायशः प्रकाशनं नामाष्टः । 73.
9. 9. इ° अ° यशोधरापुत्रजननं नाम नवमी 82a.
10. 10. इ° अ° त्रिशङ्खीष्टिकप्रव्रज्याचरणपरिवर्तनो नाम दशमः । 84b.
11. 11. इ° अ° त्रिशय्यायजूकप्रव्रज्याचरणं न° एकदः । 86b.
12. 12. इ° अ° मैत्रायणीपुत्रप्रव्रज्याचरणपरिवर्तनो न° द्वादशः । 89.
13. 13. इ° अ° अ° नालकमुनिचर्याव्रतचरणपरिवर्तनो नाम त्रयोदशी सध्यायः । 99b.
14. 14. इ° समिकसीगतशासनव्रताचरणपञ्चमः चतुर्दशः । 107b.

16. 16. इ° दिवीदासप्रवीधनकर्षकप्रव्रज्यव्रताचरणो न° षोडशः । 136.
17. 17. इ° गांगेयनाविकप्रव्रज्याव्रताचरणो न° सप्तदशः । 140b.
18. 18. इ° स्थूलबिल्लादित्रयोदशशतप्रव्रज्यव्रताचरणो न° अष्टादशः । 149.

162------------------------------------------------

20. इ° विस्विसारनराधिपसमागमपरिवर्त्तो न° विश्रुतिम्°  
160.

21. इ° सपञ्चशतपरिवाल[१. ०२००]-शालीपुत्र-महामौद्ग-  
ल्यायन-दीर्घनखप्रव्रज्यासंवरानुचरणपरिव° न° एकविं° 168b.

22. इ° आनन्दप्रव्रज्याचरणजेतवनविहारनिर्मितो न°  
द्वाविं° 172.

23. इ° काश्यपप्रव्रज्याव्रताचरणो न° त्रयोविं° 179.

24. इ° नरदन्ताद्यर्षिगणप्रव्र° चतुर्विं° । 187b.

25. इ° पितापुत्रसमागमपरिवर्त्तो न° पञ्चविं° 207b.

26. इ° राजलभद्रपिहबोधनो न° षड्विं° । 219.

27. इ° राजलभद्रकर्मविपाकपरिव° न° सप्त° । 225b.

28. इ° मैत्रकन्यावदानं न° अष्टाविं° । 234b.

29. इ° सुधनकिन्नरजातकावदानपरिव° । 249b.

30. इ° कुशसुदर्शनापरिव° विंशुतिमः । 302b—303.

31. इ° सुप्रियसार्थवाहजातकपरिव° एक । 325.

32. इ° माहपोषहस्तिजातकपरिव° द्वाविं° । 332.

33. इ° एकशुद्धमुनिलिनीकाश्यपकन्याजातप° षड्विं° । 337.

34. इ° सौदासीद्धरणे सुतसोमजातकं न° चतुर्विं° । 356b.

Compare Cārya-Pitaka Tales 25, 32 and Jātaka-mālā (Add. 1415),  
Tale 32.

35. इ° सुन्दरनन्दादिपञ्चशतशक्यकुमारप्रव्रज्याव्रतसंचा-  
रणो न° पंच° । 361b. (see above).

36. इ° सकलानन्द जन्मराजलभद्रप्रव्रज्याव्रतचारणो न°  
षट्विं° । 368b—369.

37. संबुद्धधर्मानुभावपरिव° न° सप्तविं° । 376.

38. इ° सकलानन्दाभिषेकशुद्धीधनतपोवनाभिगमनप्रवर्त्तो  
नामाष्टाविंशुतिमो ऽध्यायः समाप्तः ।

Colophons in Sanskrit and vernacular follow, giving the date and  
stating that the MS. was written by Niramuni and his son Jinendra,  
at the Yampivihāra in Lalitapur.

Add. 1415.

Paper; 130 leaves, 7 lines,  $16 \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. s. 757 (A.D. 1637).

JĀTAKA-MĀLĀ.

Contains 34 Jātakas, and purports to be the composition of Āryačūra. (Cf. *Journ. Asiatique*, May, 1875, pp. 413—417.)

Begins :

श्रीमन्ति सद्गुणपरिग्रहमङ्गलानि  
कीर्त्यास्यदान्यनवगीतमनोहराणि ।  
पूर्वप्रजन्मसु मुनेश्चरिताङ्गुतानि  
भक्त्या स्वकायकुसुमाञ्जलिनाचयिष्ये [१. ०।३०] ॥

The titles are as follows :

1. 1. इति व्याघ्रीजातकं प्रथमं । 4.
2. 2. इ० शिविजातकं द्वितीयं । 8b.
3. 3. इ० कुल्माषपिण्डीजातकं तृतीयं । 10b.
4. 4. इ० श्रेष्ठीजातकं (*sic*) चतुर्थं । 13.
5. 5. इ० अविस्मज्यश्रेष्ठिजातकं पञ्चमं । 15b.
6. 6. इ० शशजातकं षष्ठमं । 19.
7. 7. इ० अगस्त्यजातकं सप्तमं । 23.
8. 8. इ० मैत्रीवलजातकमष्टमं । 29.
9. 9. इ० विश्वन्तरजातकं नवमं । 38.
10. 10. इ० मच्चजातकं दशमं । 41b.
11. 11. इ० शक्रजातकमेकादशं (*sic*) । 43b.
12. 12. इ० ब्राह्मणजातकं दादशमं । 45b.
13. 13. इ० उन्मादयन्तीजातकं त्रयोदशमं । 50.
14. 14. इ० सुपारगजातकं (*sic*) चतुर्दशमं । 54.
15. 15. इ० मत्स्यजातकं पञ्चदशमं । 55b.
16. 16. इ० वर्त्तकापोतकजातकं षोडशमं । 56b.

1. 17. इ० कुंभजातकं सप्तदशमं । 58b.
2. 18. इ० अपुत्रजातकमष्टादशमं । 60b.

5. 21. इ० बुद्धबोधिजातकमेकविंशतमं (*sic*) । 70b.

8. 24. इ० महाकपिजातकं चतुर्विंशतितमं । 89b.
9. 25. इ० शरभजातकं पञ्चविंशतिमं । 92.
10. 26. इ० रुरुजातकं षड्विंशतिमं । 96.
11. 27. इ० महाकपिजातकं सप्तविंशतिमं । 99b.
12. 28. इ० चान्तिजातकमष्टाविंशतिमं । 104b.
13. 29. इ० ब्रह्मजातकमेकोनचिंशत्तमं । 109.
14. 30. इ० हस्तिजातकं चिंशत्तमं । 113.
15. 31. इ० सुतसोमजातकमेकचिंशत्तमं । 122.
16. 32. इ० त्रयोयुहजातकं द्वाचिंशत्तमं । 126.
17. 33. इ० महिषजातकं त्रयस्त्रिंशत्तमं । 128.
18. 34. इ० \*शतपत्रजातकं चतुस्त्रिंशत्तमं समाप्तमिति । क-  
    तिरियमार्थशूरपादानां† । सप्तैषुमुनिवत्सरिति । १५००० ।

As only the words *Kṛtir iyam Āryaṭūrāpādā-* are written in the page, and the rest is written in a somewhat more recent hand vertically on the margin, this MS. may be the original from which the MS. 95 in the Bibl. Nationale was copied ; see Feer, *Journ. As.* 1.c. p. 413.

---

\* The MS. had originally **जतपत्र०**, but the **ज** has been corrected in the margin to **श** (which agrees with the narrative); the Paris MS. reads curiously **जगतपत्र०**; see Feer, *Journ. As.* *ibid.* p. 415.

† M. Feer reads this name as 'l'auguste Āryapāda'; but it seems safer to take it as Āryaṭūra with the honorific addition of *pāda* in the plural; cf. the mention in the *Sāhitya-darpaṇa*, p. 23, of the author's great-great-grandfather as *Nārāyaṇa-padaīḥ*; and 'Candragomi-pāda,' Add. 1164.

Add. 1416.

Paper; 21 leaves, 5 lines,  $8\frac{1}{2} \times 3$  in.; modern.

SARVA-DURGATI-PARIÇODHANA (Part 2).

The same work as Add. 1632. 2, which see.

Add. 1418.

Paper; 11 leaves, 7 lines,  $9\frac{1}{2} \times 4$  in.; modern.

KATHINĀVADĀNA.

A treatise on *vinaya*, especially on dress, etc.; not a tale or *avadāna* in the ordinary sense of the term. See Burn. *Intr.* p. 39, Hodgs. *Ess. Lit. Nep.* p. 19. This work is found at Paris (Bibl. Nat.) in MS. 98 (*Divyāvadāna-mālā*).

Begins:

यः श्रीमान् स सुरासुरैरविरतं पादारविन्द्रार्चितः सा-  
चात्\* पुण्यनिधानमङ्गलगुरुश्चिन्तामणिः सर्ववित् ।

At 7b a bhikshu Sumanā gives an enumeration of the chief requisites of ritual, etc., in short paragraphs (7b—10a), with titles such as धूप । दीप ।, etc.

Ends:

कठिनावदानं समाप्तः (then a scribe's verse).

Add. 1419.

Paper; 30 leaves, 5—7 lines,  $7\frac{1}{2} \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; XVIIIth century, with recent supply.

LOKEṢVARAÇATAKAM by VAJRADATTA.

Leaves 1—3, 17—30 are a recent copy : but the remainder of the MS. is in a square hand on paper of last century; words etc. divided in red ink. The work is a hundred verses in praise of Lokeṣvara. See R. A. S. Cat. p. 23 and Hodgson *Essay Lit. Nep.* 18.

---

\* The Paris MS. has स्पर्शात्.

166------------------------------------------------

Begins :

भास्वन्नाणिक्यभासोमुकुटभूतिनमन्नाकनाथोत्तमांगे भक्तिप्रकृ-  
सरोजासनशिरसि हसन्नालतीमालिकाभाः ॥ मौलौ मी-  
लन्मृगांका ।<sup>०</sup>

After v. 25 :

इति श्रीवज्रदत्तपादविरचिते श्रीलोकेश्वरशतके नखरूचि-  
वर्णना ।

After v. 75 :

इति भगवद्वर्णना ।

Ends :

°श्रीवज्रदत्तविरचिता श्रीलोकेश्वरशतकं समाप्तं ॥

#### Add. 1420.

Paper ; 6 leaves, 7 lines,  $7 \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in. ; modern.

#### LOKEṢVARA-PĀRĀJIKĀ.

Each leaf bears the title लोकेश्वरपाराजिका.

A dialogue between Lokeṣvara and Tārā in verse, apparently not identical with, though similar in subject to, the work at Paris mentioned under Add. 1353. I have however only examined the two MSS. apart.

Begins :

श्रीमत्योतलके तारा लोकेश्वरमधुच्छत ।

भगवच्छ्रोतुमिच्छामि विपरीतपरीक्षण ।

लोकेश्वरी ऽवदत् ।

मनुष्याणां हितार्थाय उत्पातलक्षणस्य च ।

ष्टष्टु तारे प्रव्रजामि तत्फलं शान्तिकं तथा ॥

Ends :

इति श्रीलोकेश्वर-तारा संवादे विपरीतपरीक्षण पाराजिका  
समाप्ता ।

Add. 1421.

Paper; 13 leaves, 7 lines,  $7 \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; modern ordinary Devanāgarī.

VAJRA-SŪCI by AṢVAGHOSHA.

Edited by Prof. Weber (Berlin, 1860). This is a distinct work from the treatise of the same name attributed to Āṇkarācārya, shortly to be published by the compiler of this Catalogue.

Begins, after invocation to Mañjunātha :

जगदुहं मञ्जुघीषं नवा वाक्कायचितसा ॥

Ends :

इति वज्रसूची समाप्तमिति ॥

Add. 1422.

Paper; 20 leaves, 7 lines,  $7\frac{1}{2} \times 3\frac{1}{4}$  in.; modern.

DHARMA-SAṄGRAHA, attributed to NĀGĀRJUNA (called SAPTĀBHDHĀNOTTARA in Dr D. Wright's list).

There are some marks indicating hiatus in the original MS. on 6b, 12b.

A fairly correct MS.; much more so than the India Office copy, which contains an interpolation of the period of the later mythology inserted after the first two lines. The book is a summary of the terminology of Buddhist philosophy and metaphysics, etc.

Begins :

नमो रत्नत्रयाय ॥

रत्नत्रयं नमस्कुतय सर्वसत्वहितोदयं ।

कथ्यते मोहनाशाय धर्मसारसमुच्चयं ॥

तत्र तावत्तीणि रत्नानि । तद्यथा । बुद्धः धर्मः संघश्चेति ॥

त्रीणि यानानि । आवकयानं प्रत्येकयानं महायानश्चेति ॥

सप्तविधानोत्तरपूजा । सर्वज्ञेऽत्राणुसंख्यैः (?) प्रणामैः प्रणामाभ्य-  
हमित्यादिकं वन्दना ॥ १ ॥

168------------------------------------------------

मया बालिन मूढेन etc...in the same strain for 4 verses, after which :

इति सप्तविधानोत्तरपूजा ॥ बोधिचर्यावतारादिग्रंथे लिखितं ॥ ॥ त्रीणि कुशलमूलाणि ॥ बोधिचित्तोत्पाद...etc.

Next come the 4 Brahmavihāras, the 10 Pāramitās, and so on.

Ends with an enumeration of the 3 Çikshās; after which :

इति नागार्जुनपादविरचितायां (1. ० तोऽयं?) धर्मसंग्रहः समाप्तमिति ॥

#### Add. 1423.

Paper; 8 leaves, 7 lines, 7 × 3 in.; XVIIIth cent.

#### AMOGHAPĀÇA-LOKEŚVARA-PŪJĀ.

A tantric manual of devotion, etc.

There are several works extant in the Chinese Tripitaka in honour of Amoghapāça.

Begins :

ओं नमः श्रीअमोघपाशाय । ह्रीं कारसंभवंनाथं करुणा-  
क्षीरधमानसं । अमोघपाशनामाना लोकनाथं नमामि अहं ।

Ends :

ओं वज्रमण्डलम् । इति अमोघपाशलोकेश्वरपूजा

#### Add. 1424.

Paper; 4 leaves (and cover), 7 lines, 7 × 3 in.; XVIIIth cent.

#### MAÑJUGHOSHA-PŪJĀVIDHI.

Same hand as Add. 1423.

A work of tantric devotion, in very corrupt Sanskrit.

Begins :

ओं नमो मञ्जुघोषाय । ओं श्रीमत्सतगुरत्यादि (?) । शस-  
धरमिव श्रुतं खड्गपुष्टं गोपाणासु रुचिरमतिशान्तं..... ।

Ends :

इति अरयच्चन मञ्जुघोषपूजाविधि समाप्त ॥

TANTRIC DEVOTIONS.

Add. 1449.

Coarse brown paper; 24 leaves, folded backwards and forwards, 5—6 lines, 7 × 3 in.; modern.

DHĀRAṆIS, STOTRAS, &c.

Various prayers &c., in several hands, all barbarous in form and language.

Add. 1451—53.

Paper; XVIIIth cent.

TANTRIC FRAGMENTS.

170------------------------------------------------

1452. 6 leaves folded backwards and forwards, 6 lines,  $7 \times 2\frac{1}{2}$  in. Devotions, partly vernacular; ejaculations to Avalokiteśvara and others.

1453. 7 leaves, 5 lines,  $7\frac{1}{2} \times 3$  in. Fragment (leaves 5—11) of the Aikajātā-dhāraṇī.

#### Add. 1454.

Paper; 2 leaves, 5 lines,  $8 \times 3$  in.; dated N. s. 927 (A.D. 1807).

#### FRAGMENT of the STUTI-DHARMA-ĀNTU (?).

Ejaculation of praise to Avalokiteśvara and the Sukhāvatiloka, chiefly interesting from containing the exact date and place of writing, which latter occurs thus:

दानपति नेपालमण्डले भक्तापुरमहानगरे कथन्दुटोल पसु-  
पतिमहाविहार उत्तरादिगृहे स्वस्थित काश्यप गोत्र वज्राचा-  
र्यश्री भाजधनकस्य... ॥

#### Add. 1455—56.

Paper; modern writing.

#### TANTRIC FRAGMENTS.

1455. 3 leaves, 5 lines,  $7\frac{1}{2} \times 2\frac{1}{2}$  in. Fragment of a dhāraṇī or stotra, partly corrupt Sanskrit, partly vernacular.

1456. 1 leaf, 6 lines,  $8 \times 3$  in. Part of a tantra, chiefly vernacular.

#### Add. 1460.

Thick paper; 50 leaves, 5 lines,  $11 \times 3$  in.; dated N. s. 792 (A.D. 1672).

#### PAṄCARAKSHĀ.

The beginning, and the ends of the chapters, are substantially the same as in Add. 1325 (which see).

171------------------------------------------------

After the title at the end, 49a l. 5, follows the verse ये धर्मो . . , and invocations of blessing on teachers and parents in terms nearly identical with those used in Add. 875 (which compare). Then follows, (49b, l. 4) :

श्रीकाष्ठमण्डपमहानगराधिपति . . . . . (titles) जयप्रतापमल्ल-  
देव पुत्राथकरस्य विजयराज्ये ॥ दानप्रति-च्छकलिलर-वज्रमिर  
महाविहारे शारंशा (?) महाविहाराधिष्ठित-वायुव्यकन-गृहा-  
धिवासित-माता वसमुणिलक्ष्मी तस्य प्रथमपुत्रवज्राचार्य श्री  
ज्ञानचन्द्र तस्य नारि (sic) रशमि-लक्ष्मी तस्य द्वीतिय (?) पुत्री  
कन्यप्रदान ज्ञानलक्ष्मी लक्ष्मी । ते सहानुमतेन राजमानस्य  
श्राहारायोग्य-कामनायं जनध्वनसन्ताणवृधिरस्तु (sic) । दानप्रति  
उत्रायंवाडी (?) स्वगृहे प्राप्तेन धर्मचित्रजय लपाडा भगवति  
श्री ३ पञ्चरत्नामन्वीद्वारण पुस्यक चौचका । तत्पुण्यानुभावेने-  
हलोकसुखसंपत्तिधनलाभ-परलोकमुपारं प्राप्नुवन्निति । ख-  
स्मिसम्बत\*७८१ (exact day, etc.) लिखितेयं मंडूवरतलमूलमहा-  
विहारे वन्तावाहारननियताखण्डचौकच्छे (?) वज्राचार्यश्रीर-  
त्नदेयेन लिखायितं । यथा दृष्ट्वा° (a scribe's verse).

#### Add. 1464.

Palm-leaf; 227 leaves (numbered in letters and figures);  
5—6 lines, 21 × 2 in.; Kuṭila character; dated 5th year of  
Maoīpāla of Bengal (circa, A.D. 1020).

#### ASṢṬASAHAŚRIKĀ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

This MS., which is in excellent preservation, has several illustrations—chiefly of Buddhas, showing various *muḍrās* etc.—both near the beginning and end of the MS. and on the binding boards.

On the date and palæography see the Introductions. There are several glosses (e.g. at 193—4) in a hand from which it would appear that the MS. remained in Bengal many centuries after it was written.

The last leaf is much obliterated, but the subscriptions are in the same form as those of Add. 1688; in that beginning with the formula देयधर्मोऽयं, the name mentioned also bears some resemblance, बज्रभूतिदुहित-लाडाका (cf. राज्ञी उद्गाका Add. 1688).

172------------------------------------------------

The colophon runs thus :

परमेश्वरपरमभट्टारकपरमसौगतमहाराजाधिराजश्रीमन्-  
महीपालदेव प्रवर्द्धमानविजयराज्ये सप्तत् ज अश्विनिकृष्णे... ॥

On the work see Add. 866.

### Add. 1465.

Palm-leaf; originally 299 leaves (see below), 7 lines, 13 × 2 in.; dated N. s. 384 (?) (A.D. 1264).

### ASHTASAHASRIKĀ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

Leaves 32, 46, 108, 110, 145—147, 188, 191, 193, 197, 211, 238—243, 245, 297, are missing.

There are also two leaves which belong to other places, or to other MSS., numbered 113 and 11...(last fig. indistinct): there evidently however is some confusion in this part of the MS., as the real number 113, formerly placed elsewhere, and 114 do not quite fit (see the passage in 866, 79a l. 6 fin.) and moreover leaves 118—121 were originally numbered 116—119. There are also several other corrections of pagination.

The date is somewhat doubtful; it is expressed in letter-numerals but not on the usual system. The first figure is clearly चि so that the digits are expressed each by a unit-figure, not, as usual, with separate notation for tens and hundreds. The middle number is of strange form. It may be meant for ट (5), or for ह or ङ (8)\*—though it more resembles the syllable ह. The latter value is however preferable on chronological grounds, as regards the king (Abhaya-malla) named. (See Hist. Intro.) The last figure is certain. As in Add. 866, the number of verses in each chapter is given at the end. On the work see Add. 866.

The colophon, after some verses in praise of the book and the verse ये धर्मा<sup>०</sup>, runs thus :

देयधर्मी ऽयं प्रवरमहायानयायिनः परमोपाश्रिक-श्रीरा-  
मपासिडख (?) यदत्रपुण्यं... etc. ।

\* See Bhagwanlāl in the Indian Antiquary, vi. 46.

सम्मतं चिह्नं फ्राकार्त्तिकशुक्लादतीयाया आदित्यवासरे राजाधिराजपरमेश्वरश्रीमदभयमल्लदेवस्य राज्ये लिखितमिति ॥

Add. 1469.

Paper; 153 leaves, 13 lines, 13½ × 7 in.; modern.

MADHYAMA-SVAYAMBHŪ-PURĀṄA (also called SVAYĀMBHŪT-PATTIKATHĀ), with vernacular translation.

Begins:

श्रीमता येन सद्भूमस्त्रिलोके संप्रकाशितः ।  
श्रीघनं तं महाबुद्धं वन्दे ऽहं शरणश्रितः ॥  
नला त्रिजगदीशानं धर्मधातुं जिनालयम् ।  
तत्स्वयंभूसमद्वेशं वक्ष्यामि शृणुतादरात् ॥  
अद्भुया यः शृणोतीमां स्वयंभूत्यन्तिसत्कथाम् ।  
परिशुद्धत्रिकायः स बोधिसत्त्वो भवेद् ध्रुवम् ॥  
तद्यथाभूत्युरा विज्ञाजयश्रीः सुगतात्मजः ।  
बोधिमण्डपविहारे [स] विजहार ससाधिकः ॥°

175------------------------------------------------

Add. 1473.

Paper; 16 leaves, 7 lines,  $9\frac{1}{2} \times 4$  in.; modern Devanāgarī hand.

ÇAÇA-JĀTAKĀVADĀNA (Northern redaction in verse).

This is a different redaction of the story both from the Čačajātaka of the Avadāna-mālā (Add. 1415) and from that of the collection of Add. 1598. Its distinctively *northern* character is shown by the cosmogony referred to on 2a, l. 1, where Ādi-buddha is mentioned.

The MS. begins with an invocation to Padmakara of two verses.

Then a dialogue ensues between Upagupta and Ačoka, thus:

अथोपगुप्तः प्रोवाच अशोकं गुरुद्वमानसं ।

चतुरशीति साहस्यं यत्रवणलालसं ॥

शृणु राजन् त्रिरत्नांशलीके शस्यकथां (sic) गुरुभां ।

यस्योपौषधभावेन ब्रह्माद्याः सकलाः सुराः ॥

The actual story of the hare is only reached on leaf 8b, and from here to the end it will be seen that the verses of the (prose and verse) story in the Jātaka-mālā are to be found in our MS.

Thus v. 1 (p. 59 ed. Fausböll, "Five Jātakas" 1861) is to be found on 9a l. 1, and the last verse on 15b l. 1.

The dramatis personae (which may be compared with the list in the Pali tale, Fausb., "Five Jātakas," p. 58, l. 9) are thus given (15b—16):

यो ऽसौ अशोक[स]महासवो भगवान् स मुनीश्वरः ।

शारीपुत्रः शृंगाली ऽभून्नीद्रल्यो वानरी ऽभवत् ।

उद्ग्र आनन्द एवासीच्छस्यति स्वहृत्प्रियः ॥

Ends :

इति शास्त्रार्हतादिष्टं श्रुताशोकः स भूपतिः ।

तथेति प्रतिविज्ञाप्य प्राभ्यनन्दत्स पार्षदः ॥

इति अशजातकावदानं समाप्तं ॥

Add. 1475.

Paper; originally 114 leaves (see below), 5 lines, 12 × 2 in.; xviii<sup>th</sup> century.

PAṄCARAKSHĀ.

Leaves 17, 29, 92 and 104 are wanting.

The last leaf is written on different paper and in a slightly more recent hand than the rest. It contains the date N. s. 802 (A. D. 1682); but there is every reason to believe that it is simply a fresh copy of leaf found to be damaged. See Introduction, and compare Add. 1644.

The postscript consists of the verse **ये धर्मा°** etc. and the following note :

**खस्सुसंस्वत् ८°२ माहाराजद्विराज** (*sic*) **चक्रचूडामणि श्री-  
श्रीश्रीप्रथितुन्द्रमल्लदेव मयूरच्छत्र-खड्गशीर्षिकामनाथं (?) नृपति  
याकूरधर्माचक्रविजयराज्ये**

For chapters etc. see Add. 1325; but the ending of ch. 1 seems to have been on the missing leaf 17.

Two leaves (numbered 1 and 38) of a Tantric Sūtra, size and writing similar to the supply-leaf mentioned above, have been used probably as covers. The forms are barbarous throughout. Leaf 1 begins, after salutation to Gaṇeṣa and the Ratnatraya,

**एवं मया श्रुतम्यकस्मिंसमय भगवानः राजगृहे विहरति...**

Mystic syllables follow soon after.

Add. 1476.

Black paper; 29 leaves, 5 lines, 9 × 3 in.; late xviii<sup>th</sup> or xviii<sup>th</sup> cent.

DHĀRAṄIS.

The leaves are numbered 3—31. Written in gold-coloured letters.

There are illustrations of the deities to whom the dhāraṇis are addressed.

177------------------------------------------------

Leaves 3—9 contain the *Mahāpratisarā-dhārā*<sup>o</sup>.

„ 10, 11 „ *Mahāsāhasrapramardinī-dhā*<sup>o</sup>.

„ 12—16b „ *Mahāmāyūrī-dhā*<sup>o</sup>.

„ 16b—18b „ *Mahaçitavati-dhā*<sup>o</sup>.

„ 18b—20b „ *Mahāmantrānusāranī-dhā*<sup>o</sup>.

At 21a the names of the preceding Dhāraṇīs are recapitulated: then (till 22a) *Abhaya-kali* (?) *dhāraṇī*.

Leaves 22b to the end contain the *Āryā-tārā-dhāraṇī*.

### Add. 1478.

Paper; 166 leaves, 6—8 lines, 13½ × 2 in.; chiefly Bengali hand; XIV—XVth cent.

#### ÇIKSHĀ-SAMUCCAYA by JAYADEVA.

The earlier leaves have been renumbered; we have 14 and 14\*, but no 18. The writing is Bengali, with several antique features, e.g. medial *i* written as a simple curve above its consonant, not before it. 122a med. to 132a are written in a hooked-top Nepalese hand, with some early forms of letters; e.g. that of **घ**.

This MS. is the archetype of the Hodgson MS. (No. 15) in the India Office.

From the reference to the work in Wassiliew's *Tāranāth*, p. 208, it would seem that the work was compiled by Jayadeva in or about the 7th cent. A. D.

The work is a compendium of Buddhist teaching on *vinaya* and practical religious duty. It abounds in quotations, the chief sources of which are given below.

Often the quotation is not fully given and the word **पेयालं**, or abbreviated **पे** (Pāl. *peyyālam*), placed to mark the omission.

Works cited are:

*Ratnolkādhāraṇī* 2.

*Gaṇḍavyūha-sūtra* 2, 4, 73b, 76, 86, 137.

*Daçadharma-sūtra* 3b.

*Niyatāniyatāvatāra-s*<sup>o</sup> 4b, 49.

*Tathāgataguhyakā* 5b, 64b, 107b, 141, 163.

*Çūraṅgama-s*<sup>o</sup> 6.

*Bhadrakalpika-s*<sup>o</sup> 6.

इति बोधिचित्तानुशंसाः।६। (=1) 155.  
इ° पापदेशना द्वितीयःपरिच्छेदः।१°। 157.  
बोधिचित्तपरिग्रही नाम तृतीयः परिच्छेदः।११। 158.  
बोधिचित्ताप्रमादी नाम चतुर्थः परिच्छेदः।१२। 160.  
इति संप्रजन्मरचणः (*sic*) पञ्चमः परिच्छेदः।१३। 163.  
इ° चान्तिपारमिता षष्ठः परिच्छेदः।१४। 165b.  
इ° वीर्यपारमिता सप्तमः प°।१५। 170b.  
इ° ध्यानपारमिता नामाष्टमः प°।१६। 176.  
इ° प्रज्ञापारमिता नाम नवमः प°।१७। 181.  
इति बोधिचर्यावतारानुशंसावदानं।६॥ 183b.  
10. इत्यहोरात्रव्रतचैत्यसेवानुशंसावद°§ स°।१°। 194b.

\* Cf. Burn. *Intr.* p. 374 note.

† Burn. *Intr.* p. 415, note.

‡ Cf. *ibid.*, p. 425.

§ A smaller work called *Ahorātravratakathā* exists in the R. A. S. Lond.; another also (uncertain what) at Paris.

183------------------------------------------------

11. **इ° सप्तकुमारिकावद° स°।११। 207.**

This is the same tale as the separate work of the same name in prose and verse at Paris, described by Burn. *Intr.* p. 556. Copies appear to exist also in Calcutta.

12. **इति भवलुम्बकावदानं समाप्तं।१२। 215.**

The same story as *Avadāna-sāra-samuccaya* No. 4, Add. 1598, 43—56.

13. **इ° पुष्पराश्वदानं स°।१३। 218b.**14. **इ° अष्टिमहाजनावद° स°।१४। 224b.**

The story of kings Ajātaçatru, Prasena-jit and a *Çreshthin*.

15. **इ° दिव्यान्नप्रदानावद° स°।१५। 231.**

(Comp. *Avad.-Çat.* II. 10, and see Feer in *Journ. Asiatique*, 1879, XIV. p. 164).

16. **इ° वलुम्बरावदानं स°।१६। 236b.**

Cf. *Avad.-Ç.* III. 10; Feer, p. 166.

17. **इ° जाम्बालावद° स°।१७। 242b.**

See another form of the tale in *Avad.-Çat.* v. 10; Feer, p. 172.

18. **इ° हंसावद°।१८। 247b.**

See *Avad.-Ç.* vi. 10; Feer, p. 175.

19. **इ° मत्स्यपताकावद°।१९। 255.**

See *Avad.-Çat.* VII. 10; Feer, p. 177.

20. **इ° गर्गरस्यावद°। 263.**

This is the story of Virūpā, who had formerly stood at the churn (गर्गर) *Avad.-Çat.* VIII. 10; Feer, p. 180.

21. **इ° राक्षपात्तावद° स°।२१। 270.**

See *Avad.-Çat.* IX. 10; Feer, p. 184. All the three episodes of previous births are narrated and at somewhat greater length than in the *Avad.-Çat.*

22. **इ° शक्रच्यवनाद° स°।२२। 276b.**

This tale describes Indra in his glory in heaven, but feeling apprehension as to the ending of his present power and life. After a long conference with Çacī it is decided to seek a remedy in Jambūdvīpa. A troop of çramaṇas are seen, and Indra subsequently applies in person to the Buddha himself, who reinstates the god in

184------------------------------------------------

his glory. On the church's enquiring the reason of this act, the Buddha explains that on the death of the ancient Buddha Krakucchandra, a king named Çobha had built him a fine stûpa; for this he had obtained his desire of being deified; but his godhead lasted only for a time: the Buddha had therefore reinstated him, the Buddha having been born as Krakucchandra, and Indra having been Çobha.

23. **इ० पुण्यसेनावद० स० । 283.**

Some merchants' sons from Pātaliputra, in quest of sandal, apply to Punyasena; their subsequent adventures, visit to the Buddha, and meeting with a king Candraloka and others, are described.

24. **इ० भवशर्मावद० स० । २४ । 288b.**

A Brahman, Çrutavarman, has a son Bhavaçarman by his wife Jayasenā. Bhavaçarman grows up: his amorous experiences are described; he is detected in breaking into a palace; repenting of his evil ways, he reforms, practises various penances, and finally becomes a convert.

25. **इ० मधुरस्वरावद० स० । २५ । 295b.**

Birth and education of Madhurasvara, son of Sudhîra and Sumetrā. Ānanda comes to beg at the house and induces Madhurasvara to follow him; he is however permitted to return after a while to take leave of his parents. Ānanda then sends him forth on his wanderings as a bhikshu. He falls among thieves, whom he converts and ordains. Subsequent conversion and rejoicing of Sudhîra.

26. **इति पद्मकावद० स० । २६ । 302.**

Padmaka, son of Mānasa a merchant of Çrāvasti, sees the bhikshu Upasena. Padmaka gains his father's consent to his becoming a bhikshu. The first house at which he begs is that of a courtesan, Çaçilekhā; her attempted seductions; to which Padmaka turns a deaf ear and goes away with empty bowl. Çaçilekhā, however, by the help of another *veçyā*, a chaṇḍālī, Mantrabalā, tries to draw him back by love-mantras, etc. This attempt also is unsuccessful; and Padmaka succeeds in converting both women. They are received by the Buddha at Çrāvasti: on the bhikshus' enquiring the reason of this favourable reception, the Buddha explains that formerly by Kāçyapa's preaching a man, Mitra, and his maids, Nandā and Sunandā, had been converted; on their going to the sisterhood they were abused by one of the sisters; she was born, for a punishment, as a Chaṇḍālī *veçyā*;

185------------------------------------------------

Padmaka is Mitra. Another birth of Padmaka was as Pushpasena, who had met a Buddha in a flower-garden. Açoka and the rest discourse for a while on this story.

27. इ° अशोकाव° दुर्गतिपरिशोधनधारणीमण्डलप-  
रिवर्त्तीऽध्यायः स°

Scene in Indra's heaven. Buddha addresses the Devas, Kinnaras etc. Alarm of Indra. Buddha, after some conversation, reassures him and his followers, telling them, inter alia, of the *Sarvaduryati-çodhanadhāraṇī*\*: afterwards, he proclaims the samādhi called Vajrādhiṣṭhāna; recital of the *Guhyaḥṛdaya* and of other dhāraṇis and mantras. Buddha also addresses the Lokapālas and other mythological personages.

Ends :

इति श्री अशोकावदानमाला समाप्ता ॥

In the same covers is a leaf, blank on one side and perhaps intended as a cover, containing 9 numbered verses on the results of actions, etc.

Add. 1483.

Paper; 178 leaves, 9 lines,  $15 \times 4\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. s. 901 (A.D. 1781); good Devanāgari hand.

MADHYAMAKA-VṚITTI (called VINAYA-SŪTRA, see below)  
by CANDRAKĪRTI.

There are a few marks of lacunae or the like in the archetype. On the work see Burn. *Intr.*, 559 et al.; Hodgson, *Ess. Lit. Nep.* 20.

It consists of 27 chapters (प्रकरण), corresponding to the 27 topics enumerated by Csoma (= Feer in *Ann. Mus. Guim.* ii. 207).

Begins :

यो ऽत्र दयावासविधूतवासः संबुद्धधीसागरलक्ष्यजन्मा ।

सद्धर्मतो यस्य गंभीरभावं यथानुबुद्धं क्षपया जगाद ॥ १ ॥

यस्य दर्शनतेजांश्चि... etc.

नागार्जुनाय प्रणिपत्य तस्मै तत्कारिकाणां विवृतिं करिष्ये ।

\* See Add. 1623.

उत्तानसत्प्रक्रियावाक्यनद्धां तर्कानिल[*T*]याकुलितं (leg.  
लव्य°) प्रसन्नां ॥ ४ ॥

Then follow the “quelques lignes d'introduction” (five in our MS.) of which Burnouf (l. c.) speaks.

The text of the sūtra itself begins with the words :

अनिरोधमनुत्यादमनुगच्छेदमशाश्रयं । अनेकार्थमनागम-  
मनिर्गमं ॥

On which the commentary thus commences :

यः प्रतीयमनुत्यादमित्यादि ।

अत्र अनिरोधाद्यष्टविशेषणविशिष्टप्रतीयसमुत्यादः शास्त्रेऽभि-  
धेयार्थस्य सम्भवात् ॥

veka in 10.

### Add. 1485.

Black paper with gold letters; 39 leaves, 5 lines, 9½ × 3 in.; dated N. S. 797 (A.D. 1677).

A collection of DHĀRANĪS, etc.

The leaves are numbered 16—54. The hand is of the square, close, and somewhat illegible character usual in MSS. of this material.

The contents are as follows :

1. *Prajñāpāramitāhṛdayam*.

The Chinese version of this work is translated by Prof. Beal, Journ. R. A. S. 1875, p. 27. The identity of the works will be seen by comparing with his version the opening sentences of the sūtra itself.

Begins :

तेन खलु पुनः समयेनार्थावलोकितेश्चरी बोधिसत्त्वा महा-  
सत्त्वः गम्भीरायां प्रज्ञापारमितायां चर्यामेवं व्यवलोकयती (sic)  
स्म। पञ्च स्कन्धान्स्वभावशून्यान् व्यवलोकयती स्म।

and, a few lines further on :

रूपं शून्यं शून्यतैव रूपं न रूपात्पृथक् शून्यता न शून्यता-  
पृथक् रूपं। वेदना शून्यं etc.। संज्ञा शून्यं...। संस्कारा शून्यं  
...। विज्ञानं शून्यं...। एवं भदन्त शारिपुत्र सर्वधर्माः स्वभा-  
वशून्या अलक्षणान् अनुत्यन्ता अनिरुद्धाः...

The words supplied by Prof. Beal on conjecture (p. 28) thus appear (18, l. 2) : गते गते पारंगते पारसंगते बोधि स्वाहा।

The sūtra then concludes with a few lines such as are usually found in these works.

Nos. 2—6 are the *Dhāraṇīs*; the same, and in the same order as in R. A. S. MS. 79, Nos. 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 : viz. the *dhāraṇīs* called *Vajravīdūraṇī* (20b), *Gaṇapatīhṛdaya* (22b), *Uṣṇīṣhavijaya* (25b), *Māricī* (29), and *Grahamātrikā* (38).

7. *Pratyañjira-dhāraṇī* (52b).

See R. A. S. Cat. No. 77.

एतद्दिने लिखितं... काष्टमण्डपनगरे... श्रीरत्नवज्रिण (?)...।

The letters are much faded, especially towards the beginning, and accordingly the last few pages, and some other passages, have been retraced with modern ink. Preface, etc. as in printed editions.

Ends :

इत्यमरसिंहकृतो नामलिङ्गानुशासने अमरकोषः परिसमाप्तः।

127b. Then follows a short summary of contents, after which :

अथोऽसु श्रीमन्नेपालिकसम्बन्धरे। \*सम्बत ५०० मार्गः\*  
शुद्ध श्रीपञ्चम्या तिथौ रैवतिनक्षत्रे साध्ययोगे सोमवासरे  
श्रीमत ललितापुरि निवासित... (here an erasure)... सितः।  
पुषा नाम मडापात्र श्री (erasure) त्यगुणम सिंह महापात्र  
श्रीरघुसिंहदेवानामध्यनार्थं पृष्ठकममरसिंह लिखापितः।  
वज्राचार्यश्री जसचन्द्रेण लिखितमिति। यथा दृष्टं etc. ||

On the back, in a slightly larger, but probably contemporary hand :

श्रीनेपालसमधिविन्दुविशये माघासिते मासे श्रे (?)। पञ्चम्या  
तिथि चित्तससि दिने यत (?) गन्द (read ०ण्ड) योगातिता।

This seems to be for *nepāla sam[vaṭ] abdhi-vindu-vishaye* = 504 = A.D. 1384. *Vindu* as a word-numeral is not known : but in these MSS. the sign of zero is frequently a mere dot, like anusvāra (*vindu*). The king's name is next given :

नेपालाभुवमण्डलेश्वर प्रभूपते...। देवश्रीजयार्जुनो (*sic*)  
हि... परे लोकात्मम् (? 1. त०) भूपति।

This date (1384) will accord with MSS. Add. 1689 and 1395; in the former of which we find Jayārajuna (or ॐjāna) reigning in A.D. 1374, and in the latter, Jayasthiti in 1385. Compare the nearly coeval date in the note to Add. 1544.

#### Add. 1533.

Paper; 18 leaves, 6 lines, 7½ × 3 in.; XVIIIth cent.

AṆVAGHOŠA-NANDIMUKHĀVADĀNA.

---

\*...\* This is written over in quite modern ink;—what was underneath is undecipherable.

191------------------------------------------------

Leaf 1 is missing.

The language is almost as hopelessly barbarous as in the other MSS. of the work (Add. 1357 et al. q. v.). The last two leaves are in a slightly different hand. A recent corrector has retouched the MS. here and there, in the vain endeavour to make the jargon into something like correct Sanskrīt. The MS. shows some distinct varieties of reading, as compared with the other copies.

A leaf at the beginning, serving as a cover, contains a fragment of the *Vasudhārā dhāraṇī*, and begins :

ॐ नमो भगवत्यै आर्यश्रीवसुधारायै ॥  
दिव्यरूपी स्वरूपी च सौम्यरूपी वरप्रदा ।  
वसुधरी वसुधारी च वसुश्रीश्रीकरिवराः ॥  
धारणी धाता...

#### MEGHA-SŪTRA.

This MS. was not used for my printed edition, owing to its not having been identified in the printed list: see Add. 1689 and R. A. S. Journal, Apr. 1880.

At the end :

लिखितेयं वस्व (?) मल्लवाहार वज्राचार्य पूरन्दरण लिखित  
संपूर्णमिति ।

#### Add. 1535.

Paper; 6 leaves, 7 lines (ruled),  $9\frac{1}{2} \times 3$  in.; XVIIIth cent.

PIṆḌAPĀTRĀVADĀNA-KATHĀ, attributed to DĪPAṆKARA.

Bad writing and full of barbarisms.

Apparently the same work as in R. A. S. Cat. No. 45, but *not* the same as Add. 1305, *supra*. It also exists in Paris, Bibl. Nat. MS. 98 ("Divyāvadāna-mālā").

192------------------------------------------------

The speaker is the Tathāgata, Dīpaṅkara; the hearer king Sarvānanda.

प्रत्येकबुद्धाय च संनिधाय पांशुप्रदानान्तिष्ठपूर्वजन्मनि।

तस्मात्प्रदानाद्भुवि श्रृङ्गकल्पे दीपावभूमौ च पतिर्वभूव ॥

तद्यथानुश्रयते। नयविनयादिमैत्रीगुणसमुदयाकीर्णे दीपा-  
वतीमहानगर्था सर्वनन्दी नाम नरपतिरासीत्।

The latter half of the work (4b-6a) consists of short paragraphs in verse on the various offerings suitable to be made to a bhikshu with a piṇḍa-pātra, etc.; and concludes (6b) with a few words on appropriate seasons, months, etc.

Ends :

इति श्रीदीपंकालतथागतस्य (sic) पिण्डपात्रावदानकथा  
समाप्ता।

#### Add. 1536.

Paper; 32 leaves, 5 lines, 9 × 3 in.; dated N. s. 803 (A. D. 1683).

SVAYAMBHŪ-PURĀṆA (smallest redaction).

See Add. 870.

Illustrations on leaves 1, 2, and 32.

Ends (with date as above, and scribe's name):

लिखितं वज्राचार्यचक्रदेवेन निति (sic).

#### Add. 1537.

Paper; 48 leaves, 5 lines, 10½ × 3 in.; modern Nepalese hand, but squarer and more regular than usual.

KAPḌĀVADĀṆA.

The leaves are numbered 1-25, 27-49.

There are words of comment, and divisions of words, occasionally written above the lines in red, or in black.

The MS. seems to be an exact reproduction of the archetype of Add. 1345, as the preface, titles, and even the concluding scribe's verse, are identical.

193------------------------------------------------

Add. 1538.

Paper; 40 leaves, 6 lines (ruled),  $14 \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. S. 964 (A.D. 1844).

VĪRAKUṢĀVADĀNA, from the DIVYĀVADĀNA.

Text very faulty. Copies at Paris and Calcutta.

This is a story in praise of the *asṭamū-vrata*, or fast on the eighth day, and probably composed to be read on solemn occasions of this kind. The subject matter purports to be taken from the *Divyāvadāna* (much of which seems to have perished), and illustrates the merit of the fast, etc.

Begins in verse thus (after invocation):

नवा गुरु चिरन् च कथयामि समाप्तः ।

अष्टमीव्रतमाहात्म्यं नैपालेभाषितं महत् ।

तद्यथाभूत्युरा विज्ञाजयः श्रीः सुगतात्मजः ।

बोधिमण्डिविहारे ऽसौ[ ] विजहार ससांधिकः ।

तत्र जिनेश्वरी नाम बोधिसत्त्वाय प्रावदत् । तं श्रुवाथ अश्री-  
काय कुक्कुटारामसंश्रितः ॥ उपगुप्तः पुनः प्राह अष्टमीव्रतमु-  
त्तमं । etc.

After a few more verses, the prose begins thus :

सर्वार्थसंज्ञकी ऽष्यसौ राजपुत्रकी भवति तदाराजा चिन्त-  
यामास...

The king observes the *asṭamūvrata* in honour of Amogha-pāṇa and Avalokiteśvara (3a. 1); and is rewarded by the miraculous birth of a son from a sugar-cane (*ikshu*), therefore called Ikshvāku. A short story is now told of a *sārtthavāha*, Supriya, who gained a shower of jewels by a *vrata* (4a-b). The king abdicates in favour of his son Ikshvāku (4b, 1-2). The queen Alindā (after due attention to the *vrata*) bears a son; his name was to have been Kuṭala, but is shortened by his mother to Kuṭa (6a, 5-6); he performs sundry deeds of prowess, whence he gains the name of Vīra-kuṭa (6b. 5). The life of Vīra-kuṭa is now detailed, and the moral is thus summed up in the last line :

वशिष्ठ च्छीना (sic) शतजन्मोपवासं कृतं तदपि मोक्षपदं न  
प्राप्तं, वीरकुशराज्ञा अष्टमीव्रतैकेन मोक्षफलं प्राप्नो ऽभूत् ॥

194------------------------------------------------

इति श्रीदिव्यावदानोद्भूत अष्टमीव्र[त]महात्म्यकूशावदानं (sic)  
समाप्तं ॥

Add. 1543.

Palm-leaf; 113 leaves, 6 lines, 12 × 2 in.; XIth cent.

SUVIKRĀNTAVIKRĀMI-PARIPṚICCHĀ,  
or SĀRDDHADVISAHASRIKĀ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

The hand is very similar to that of R. A. S. Cat. No. 2: see the plate there, and compare that in Palæographical Soc. Orient. Ser., Pl. 43. The MS. is corrected in several hands, mostly of considerable antiquity.

This redaction has 7 *parivarttas* and 2500 verses. See Wassiliew, *Taran.* 159 (147), and Csoma *As. Res.* xx. 395 (also Ann. Mus. Guim. ii. 201) as to its Tibetan form, etc.

After 15 lines of invocation and preface, the text begins (2b):

एवं मया श्रुतमेकस्मिन् समये भगवान् राजगृहे विहरति  
स्म। वैणुवने कलन्दकनिवापे महता भिचुसंधेन सार्धमर्द्धचयो-  
दश भिचुश्रुतैः....

The chapters end :

1. 1. आर्यप्रज्ञापारमितानिदाने प्रथमः। 19b.
2. 2. आर्यप्रज्ञापारमितायामानन्दपरिवर्त्तो नाम द्वितीयः।  
   24b.
3. 3. तथ्यता परिवर्त्तो नाम तृतीयः 37b.
4. 4. श्रीपम्यपरिवर्त्तो नाम चतुर्थः। 60.
5. 5. सुभूतिपरिव° पञ्चमः। 64b.
6. 6. चर्यापरिव° षष्ठः। 94.
7. 7. अनुशंसापरिव° सप्तमः। आर्यसुविक्रान्तविक्रामिपरि-  
   पृच्छाप्रज्ञापारमिता निर्देश सार्द्धदिसाहस्रिका भगवत्यार्यप्रज्ञा-  
   पारमिता समाप्ता।

Then follows the verse यः सर्वज्ञतया..., with which the other Prajñāpāramitā in 2500 verses begins (Add. MSS. 1628 and 1629). The works, however, seem distinct; see Wassiliew (supra cit.). As

195------------------------------------------------

to the subject, Suvikrāntavikrāmin's first questions of the Buddha give some notion of the general drift and are thus expressed (3a-b):

प्रज्ञापारमिताप्रज्ञापारमितेति भगवन्नुच्यते। कियता भगवन् बोधिसत्त्वानाम्हासत्त्वानाम्प्रज्ञापारमिताप्रज्ञापारमितेत्युच्यते। कथम्भगवन् बोधिसत्त्वो महासत्वः प्रज्ञापारमितायाश्चरतः प्रज्ञापारमिताभावना परिपूर्विकृच्छति। कथं भ० बोधिसत्त्वस्य महा० प्रज्ञापारमिताम्भावयतो मारः पापीयानवतारं लभते [1] सर्वमारकर्मणि चावबुध्यते। कीदृ गृपैश्च भगवन् प्रज्ञापारमिता विहारैर्विहरन् बोधिसत्त्वो महास० चिप्रं सर्वज्ञताधर्मपरिपूर्विकृच्छति।

In ch. 2, at the beginning, the speakers are Ananda and Āradvatīputra (Ārip<sup>o</sup>); in ch. 5, Āradvatīputra and Subhūti. Elsewhere they seem to be Suvikrāntavikrāmin and the Buddha.

At the end is an odd leaf, found in Add. 1680, written by the scribe of this MS. in precisely the same style, and containing quite similar matter; but it is not numbered like the rest.

#### Add. 1544.

Paper; 172 leaves, 7 lines, 20 × 2 in.; XI<sup>th</sup> cent.

#### ASHTASĀHASRIKĀ-PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

See Palæograph. Soc. Or. Series, Plate 57, where Professors Cowell and Eggeling assign the MS. to the XI<sup>th</sup> cent. Note also that the conjecture there given as to the individual peculiarity of the sloping hand is confirmed by leaves 84—87 of Add. 1708.

For the postscript see Add. 1163.

There are dates of recitation written on the back of both the first and the last leaves; the one being n. s. 492, the other 499. The last two figures of the latter are obscure: the former is interesting as being expressed both in figures and words (नेपाल समा दिनन्द जलघौ).

PAṄCA-MAHĀ-RAKSHĀ-SŪTRĀṄI.

A collection of dhāraṇīs in connexion with the same 5 Çaktis as those of the Paṇca-rakshā, Add. 1325 (which see). Spaces are left for pictures of the Çaktis at the heads of the chapters.

Add. 1580.

ḌAMBŪKĀVADĀNA.

200------------------------------------------------

The MS. gives the title with the strange barbarism **सम्भूक°**.

The style and subject of the tale are not unlike the *Pañca-tantra*, the first personages introduced being seven shells (*sambhūkāḥ*) and a fisherman named *Rūpaka*; further on, both personages and scenery are distinctively Buddhist.

Begins, after 4 *ślokas* of prefatory invocation :

आदौ तावदादिकथामुपवक्ष्येते । अस्ति हंसगधेषु चैत्यगर्भो  
नाम पुष्करणी (*sic*) सारानेकजलचलजन्तुविहगन (*sic*) निवास-  
भूता । तस्याच्च सप्त सम्भूका वस्तुवुः (?)

Ends :

सम्भूकावदाननिदानकथा समाप्तः॥ स° ५४७ अष्टुनि कृष्ण १°  
लिखित श्री डंखाचोकवज्राचार्य श्री पंडजसः (?)

Ends :

इति श्रीधर्मलक्ष्मीसंवादे अस्ती (*sic*) अध्याये समाप्तः।

#### Add. 1585.

Paper; 13 leaves, 10 lines (ruled), 14 × 3 in.; fine, minute hand-writing of the XVIIIth cent.

SUMĀGADHĀVADĀNA and part of the  
DAṆĀKUṢALAJAM PHALAM.

On the right side of each leaf (1—12) **सुमागधा**.

On the tale see Burnouf, *Intr.* p. 566, and Rājendralāla, *Nep. Buddh. Lit.* p. 237.

Begins :

नमः श्रीसर्वबुद्धबोधिसत्तेभ्यः । बुद्धो भगवान् सत्ततो गुरु-  
क्षतो मानितः राजभी राजामात्यैर्धनिभिः°

201------------------------------------------------

Ends :

**सुमागधावदानं समाप्तं । 13a.**

On 13b there is marked at the side **दशाकुशल**.

Then, after invocations to Buddha, etc. :

**प्रवक्ष्यामि दशाकुशलजं फलं । सधर्मस्तत्युपस्थाने प्रोक्ता  
भगवता स्वयं ।**

The MS. ends abruptly with this leaf.

### Add. 1586.

Paper ; 20 leaves, 10 lines (ruled), 14 × 3 in.; fine, minute hand-writing, dated N. s. 781 (A.D. 1661).

#### RĀŚṬRAPĀLA-PARIPṚICCHĀ.

The title of the work is cited in Böhtlingk and Roth from the Vyutpatti : see two citations in Add. 1478 above.

Mr Nanjio informs me that there is a Chinese translation of this work, made by Jñānagupta under the Sin dynasty (A.D. 589—618), being No. 23 (18) of the Chinese Tripitaka. On the Tibetan see Feer, in Ann. G. ii. 254. [See also the account in the Supplementary note at the end of the text.]

After the invocation, a line of preface commences with the words **भास्वत्सुद्धर्मरत्नव्यतिकरघटितां...**; then follow **एवमया श्रुतं** and a large list of Bodhisattvas etc., forming the audience.

Ends :

**पुण्यरक्षेः सत्युरुषस्व पूर्वभागसूत्ररत्नराजं समाप्तं आर्यरा-  
द्रपालपरिपृच्छा नाम महायानसूत्रं समाप्तमिति । ये धर्म<sup>०</sup> ।  
date (see above) । श्रीमहावुद्धोपाधिक श्री जयमुनि लिखितं ।**

(Newari).

ऋपां श्रीशक्यसिंह भगवानयातननमस्कालयाठाः

1. Ends:

इति श्रीविचित्रकर्णिकावदानमालायां प्रथमोऽध्यायः । 16b.

4. इ० श्री० ंदानेद्वृते नेपारभाखा चतुर्थे । (i.e. नेपालभाषा०) 65.

5. इ० अ० न० पंचमोऽध्यायः । 76b.

8. क० अ० न० अष्टमोऽध्यायः । 111.

इति श्री पप्रसेषर [= ०शेखर] अवदान समाप्तं

#### Add. 1590.

Paper ; 257 leaves, 10 lines, 13 × 4 in. ; modern.

#### KALPADRUMĀVADĀNA-MĀLĀ.

See Feer in Journ. Asiatique 1879, p. 283 ; also the list of tales, ibid., p. 304 ; and likewise the long account in Dr Rājendralāla Mitra, *Nep. Buddh. Lit.* pp. 292 sqq.

Add. MS. 900 is a copy of the last two tales.

#### Add. 1592.

Paper ; 121 leaves, 10 lines, 12 × 5 in. ; modern.

Part of the RATNĀVADĀNA-MĀLĀ (17 tales).

In Add. 1615 the work is said to be 'spoken' by Çrīghana.

For a general account see Feer, Journ. Asiatique 1879, pp. 283 sqq. ; and his full list of Tales, ibid., 305.

The titles coincide with those of the Paris MS., except where otherwise stated.

### Add. 1593.

Paper; 253 leaves, 7—8 lines, 12 × 3½ in.; in several hands, the last giving a date N. s. 939 (A.D. 1819).

### SĀDHANA-MĀLĀ-TANTRA.

Add. 1686 is a large fragment of an old MS. of the same work: see the account of that MS.

Begins :

नमो वज्रासनाय ॥ नत्वा सर्वतथागतान् गुणगणाधारान् परार्थं गुरोः श्रीवज्रासन नाम भूषित संसारदोषयहैः ॥ etc.

A little further on :

ततः पुरतो पुरतो भगवन्तं वक्ष्यमाणविधिना वर्णभुजादि समन्विते विचिन्त्य पुष्पादिकं प्रयच्छेत् ॥ श्रीं नमो ऽक्षोभ्याय हं श्रीं वज्रपुष्प etc.

204------------------------------------------------

Ends :

सर्वेवृद्धसमेषोऽशभुजस्य बलिमन्त्रः ॥ शताचरं पठेत् चमा-  
पनवलिविसर्जनः ॥ विरूपायुः ॥

Date (in semi-vernacular),

संवत् ८३८ श्रावण शुक्ल पञ्चमी कुट्टुयहितिकाथवाहालय  
(विहार) श्रीवज्राचार्ये अमृतानन्दनायजुली शुभं ॥

### Add. 1596.

Paper; 26 leaves, 6 lines,  $13\frac{1}{2} \times 3$  in.; dated N. s. 950  
(A.D. 1830).

### RATNAGUṆA-SAṄCAYA.

The leaves have the double numbering, 1—26, and (more conspicuously) 71—96, so that the present MS. would seem to have belonged to a series.

Apparently a collection of gāthās illustrative of the Ashtasahasrikā Prajñā-pāramitā; for none of them seem to occur in the text of that work, as we have it in Add. 866.

In 32 short chapters, corresponding to those of the Ashtas<sup>o</sup>.

Begins :

अथ खलु भगवोस्ताञ्चतमदाम्भर्षणार्थे (?) पुनरपीमां  
प्रज्ञापारमितां परिदि[प]यमानस् तस्मां वरयामि मालगाथा

4. भगवत्या रत्नगुणसञ्चय गाथाया सर्वाकारञ्चतचर्या-  
परीवर्त्तो नाम प्रथम अध्यायः । (74).

5. भ° र° श्रुक् प° नाम द्वितीय ।

6. भ° र° गाथायामप्रमेयगुणधारणपारमितास्त्वपसंस्कार  
नाम षट्तिथः परिव° etc.

For the other titles see Add. 866.

Ends :

आर्याष्टुष्टहस्तिकायां भगवत्या प्रज्ञापारमितायां पलिवता  
नुसारिन (*sic*) भगवत्या (?) रत्नगुणसंघयगाथायां समाप्तः ।

Then follow date, etc., as above.

205------------------------------------------------

रिषितेयं (i.e. लिखि<sup>०</sup>) काय-वाहारया श्रीवजवाक्ष्य पद्या-  
कलिासंहिन।

The above titles, etc. may serve as examples of the barbarously incorrect character of the MS.

Add. 1598.

Paper; 171 leaves, 5 lines, 11 × 4 in.; modern.

AVADĀNA-SĀRA-SAMUCCAYA.

The MS. is torn in places, and ends abruptly at the same word as Add. 1585.

Many of the leaves have on their left margin अ० सा० स०, so that I have supposed this to be part of the work No. 48 in Mr Hodgson's list (Essay Lit. Nep. p. 38).

Begins :

मूलं मलानां प्रवदन्ति जातिं जातिप्रपंचोपशमप्र वीणः ।

तथागताश्चावितथप्रतिज्ञा संबोधिसत्त्वाश्च विशुद्धसत्त्वाः ।  
तद्यथा ।

1. इति श्रीसार्थवाहजातकं समाप्तं । 22a.

2. इ० सर्वदद न० । 33a.

3. इ० मत्सरानन्दावदानं (*sic*) । 43b.

4. इ० भवलुब्धकाव० । 56b.

5. इ० वदरदीपजातकं । 63b.

6. इ० शशजातकं । 70.

7. इ० चन्द्रप्रभाजातकं । 78.

8. इ० रूपावति ज० । 85b.

9. इ० हस्तिजातकं । 101b.

10. इ० चन्द्रजातकं । 111b.

11. इ० मृगजातकं । 121a.

12. इ० मयूरजातकं । 137b.

The remainder of the MS. coincides exactly with Add. 1585, containing the Sumāghadāvadāna and the fragment there noticed.

206------------------------------------------------

Tale 1 is apparently different from the tale of the sārthavāha Dharmalabha in the Mahāvastu (Add. 1339, leaves 232—241).

Tale 2 is also different from its homonym in the Avadānakalpa-lokā (Add. 913, leaves 15—17); though the merit of sacrificing even one's body for another's benefit is a moral pointed in both.

Tale 3 is the story of a sārthavāha named Nanda, who is punished for his excessive accumulation of wealth in a former birth, by a miserable spirit of miserly jealousy (मात्सर्य) in this.

Tale 4 is the same as Açokāv. No. 12; thus the verses of lamentation uttered by the *tapasvinī* at the birth of her son, beginning जाते पुत्रे.., (49b), are identical: the bulk of both tales consists of discourses on *abhidharma*, etc. The beginning of this story however corresponds with 208a. line 4, not 207b, of Add. 1482, as the tale there has several pages of introduction and scenery.

✓ Tale 5 is the story of a sārthavāha named Supriya, in the reign of Brahmadata at Benares.

Tale 6. Vid. Add. 1473 et al. ib. cit.

Tale 7. On Candraprabha see Burn. *Intr.* 138, 160.

Tale 8. See Divyāvadāna, Tale xxxii.

Tale 9. A long tale of Brahmadata, king of Benares and his experiences in elephant-hunting: different from the tale of the same name in the Jātaka-mālā.

Tale 10. A tale illustrative of the virtue of *kshānti*, in which the chief actors are a certain Kalinga-rāja and Candrakumāra. (The Chanda-kumāra-jātaka in the Cariya-pitaka is illustrative of *dāna*.)

Tale 11. One of the numerous deer-births. In praise of *vīrya*.

Tale 12. A story of a peacock, Suvarṇāvabhāsa.

### Add. 1603.

Paper; 18 leaves, 6 lines,  $12\frac{1}{2} \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. S. 919 (A.D. 1799).

UPOSHADHĀVADĀNA, [DEVAPUTROTTPATTI,  
and DOSHANIRṆAYĀVADĀNA.

These are clearly the same works as R. A. S. No. 15 (q. v.). See also Add. 1610.

207------------------------------------------------

Begins (after invocations of the Ratnatraya and Avalokiteśvara):

एवं मया श्रु० एक० स० भगवान्कपिलवसुनि महानगर्थां  
स्वच्छ० etc.

इति सुगतवाग्निसंवादे भगवती ऽमोघपाशुलोकेश्वरस्य\*  
पोषधावदानाचरितकथायामुपोषधा नाम देवपुत्रोत्पत्तिना-  
मावदानं प्रथमं । ० । 10a.

Ends:

इति श्रीमदार्थावलोकितेश्वरस्य श्रीसुगतवाग्निसंवादे सा-  
नुमत्तजदीषनिर्णयो नामावदानं समाप्तमिति । (Date as above).

The titles अस्ति० and अष्टि० occur at the right hand of many leaves; on the cover is the general title वासिष्ठवादन.

#### Add. 1607.

Paper; 224 leaves, 6 lines,  $12\frac{1}{2} \times 3$  in.; dated N. s. 902  
(A.D. 1782).

#### SADDHARMA-LAṆKĀVATĀRA.

On the work see Add. 915, and add a reference to Feer in Ann.  
Mus. Guim. ii. 237. This MS. represents an independent text, for  
though both are often faulty, passages are preserved here (e.g. 168a,b,  
ending as Add. 915, 12a, 2) that are marked as lacunae in that MS.

#### Add. 1609.

Paper; 52 leaves folding backwards and forwards, 7 lines,  
 $9 \times 3\frac{1}{2}$  in.; XVIIIth cent.

Manual of TANTRIC PŪJĀ (BALI-PŪJĀ or CAKRA PŪJĀ ?).

The paper is mounted on muslin and gauze.

The title चक्रपूजा is given on the wrapper.

The title Bali-pūja given in Dr Wright's list is partly substan-  
tiated by the ending of the work; which runs: इति पंचबलि ...  
after 3 lines श्रेशिका बलि.

The final colophon is simply इति पूजा समाप्ता॥ ० ॥

---

\* Cf. Oxf. Cat. 388b, l. 1.

208------------------------------------------------

Add. 1610.

Paper; 14 leaves, 9 lines, 13 × 5 in.; modern.

UPOSHADHĀVADĀNA.

This is the same work as R. A. S. Cat. No. 15, which is there called "Uposhadhāvadānam and Doshanirṇayāvadānam", but the form "उपोष°" is written on the right hand of several leaves throughout our MS., showing that "Uposhadha" is a general name for the whole work. It consists of 2 adhyāyas.

This avadāna belongs to the latest style of composition. There is no continuous plot, but the work is a somewhat rambling dialogue between the Buddha, Vācishṭha and others: there are several pages of invocations etc. At 11a there occurs a short story about Dharmapāla king of Vaiṣālī. See more in Rājendralāla Mitra, *Nep. B. Lit.* p. 265.

Text begins :

एवं मया श्रु° ए° स° कपिलवस्तुनि महानगर्थां स्वच्छनि-  
र्मलसुगन्धशीतलखादुलधु°

इति भगवन्सुगतवाशिष्ठसंवादे भगवतो ऽमोघपाशलीकेश्वर-  
स्व उपोषधावदानाचरितकथायाम् उपोषधी नाम देवपुत्रो-  
त्यन्तिनाम प्रथमो ऽध्यायः। 6b-7a.

Ends :

इति सुग° व° सं° सानुमत्तजदीषनिर्णय नामावदानं  
समाप्त ॥

Add. 1611.

Paper; 98 leaves, 13 lines, 14 × 4 in.; dated N. s. 765 (A.D. 1645).

AVADĀNA-ṢATAKA.

This MS. is fully described by M. Feer in the *Journ. Asiatique* (Aug.—Dec. 1879). Cf. especially p. 144: "Ce MS. est sans doute le seul représentant des anciens manuscrits de cet ouvrage."

A full list of the tales is to be found *ibid.* p. 302. For the numbering compare p. 159, note.

209------------------------------------------------

Add. 1614.

Paper; 36 leaves, 7 lines, 13 × 4 in.; late XVIIIth cent., with modern supply.

A collection of STOTRAS and Songs.

The MS. consists of 19 leaves originally numbered 20—38, now 13—31, written in a square and regular Devanāgarī hand (perhaps of the end of the XVIIIth cent.), with lines and margins ruled; the remainder (1—12, 32, 36) is a very recent supply by Nepalese scribes.

The verses in each hymn are numbered, and the numbers are accordingly given below.

Begins :

नमः श्रीमदवलोकितेश्वराय ॥ बोधिस्वाय ॥

The following are the first occurring titles :

इत्यार्थावलोकितेश्वरभट्टारकस्य चर्पतिपादविरचितं स्तोत्रं समाप्तं ॥ २५ ॥ 3.

इत्यार्था° श्रीबन्धुदत्तत्राचार्यकृतं स्त° स° ॥ २५ ॥ 5.

इत्यार्था° रूपस्तवं समाप्तं। २४। 6b.

इत्यार्था° नरकीद्वरस्तोत्रं स°। २५। 7b.

इत्य° सप्ताचरस्त° स°। ८। ib.

इत्य° चरणदर्शनाभिलाषस्त° स°। १°। 8b.

इत्य° षड्गतिस्त° स°। ६। 9.

Short hymns to Avalokiteśvara continue till 13 (the original MS.) where we get:

इति सुगतावदानीद्वृत सप्तबुद्धस्तोत्रं समाप्तं।

and इ° प्रज्ञापारमितास्तुतिः स°। *ibid.*

The hymns next following are chiefly addressed to Çakya-siṃha, and are attributed to 'Harsha-deva-bhūpati' (15) and Yaçodharā (16, 17). Among the next following are hymns purporting to be taken from the Bhadrakalpaṭaḍāna, by Navagraha (17b), and from the Svayambhūpurāṇa (20). From 33 onwards the hymns are called 'songs' (गीत). Each consists of about a score of numbered verses, the last being entitled लोकेश्वरगीतं.

The collection has no general title or colophon.

Add. 1615.

Paper; 143 leaves, 10 lines, 11 × 6 in.; dated in words N. S. 923 (A.D. 1803); large modern Nepalese hand, often sloping to the right.

Parts of the RATNĀVADĀNAMĀLĀ and of other collections of tales.

Each tale, as a rule, has its own pagination, and on the margin are written the names of the collection and of the tale.

The tales are as follows:

I. From the *Ratnāvadāna* (see the names of the tales in Add. 1592). The scribe seems to have transcribed what he could find in an imperfect copy; so he has written it in seven portions of 24, 12, 9, 34, 11, 18, and 7 leaves, respectively, making 109 leaves in all.

II. From the *Dvāvimṣatyavadāna*, 7 leaves.

The text seems independent of that of Add. 1274. Tale 1 ends 5a; 2 ends 5b; 3 ends 6a; 4 ends 7a; 5 breaks off at 7b. This last is not the same as tale 5 in Add. 1274.

III. Work on the 12 *Tirthas* of Nepāl.

14 leaves. In verse throughout.

Text begins:

अथेदानां प्रवक्ष्यामि तीर्थानि दादशानि च ।

पृष्टं मैत्रेय पुष्पानि तीर्थानाञ्च विशेषतः ॥

Ends abruptly (14a):

दिजादय मनुष्याश्च पूर्ववत्तानदानकं ।

क्त्वा पुष्पप्रशादाच्च यशुष्टिद्विफलं लभेत ॥

IV. *Uposhadhāvadāna* (2nd adhyāya).

13 leaves, numbered 1—13, though the MS. begins somewhat abruptly at a point corresponding to 7a, l. 1 fin., of Add. 1610 (q. v.).

इति सुगतवाग्निष्ठसंवादे उपोषधनामावदानं द्वितीयो

अब्दे नैपालिके याते वक्रियमग्रहयुते ।

मासे कृष्णे माधवे च इन्दुवारे समन्विते ॥

यदि सुद्धं etc. ॥ ये धर्मा ... ॥

Add. 1617.

Paper; 55 leaves, 7 lines,  $18\frac{1}{2} \times 4$  in.; date at end N. S. 945 (A.D. 1825).

GUHYA-SAMĀJA (APARĀRDHA).

On the whole work see Add. 1365, and add a reference to the detailed account of it in Rajendralal's *Lalita Vistara*, Intr. pp. 11—16; and *Nep. B. L.*, 259. There is another copy of this part in R. A. S. Cat. No. 44.

Begins:

एवं मया श्रुतमेकस्मिं समये भगवान् तथागतकायवाक्-  
चित्तयोषिद्धने विजहार ।

1. इति महागुह्यातिगुह्ये गुह्यसमाजे ऽपराधे सर्वतन्त्रनिदा-  
नरहस्यात् श्रीसंपुटीद्भवकल्पराजः प्रथमः पटलः 8.

2. ॐ कल्पराजो द्वितीयः । 15.

3. ॐ पराधि महातन्त्रराजे हेरुकीत्यन्तिस्तृतीयः पटलः ।  
16—17.

4. ॐ राजे नैरात्म्यसाधनं नाम चतुर्थपटलः । 18.

5. ॐ संपुटीद्भवः 21.

6. ॐ वज्रडाकिनीसंकेतकल्पराजं नाम 23.

7. ॐ चर्यालिंगनः 27.

8. ॐ तन्त्रराजे अष्टमः 30.

9. ॐ वसन्ततिलकवर्णनः 34b.

10. [The title of this chapter, as in the R. A. S. MS., seems to be wanting.]

212------------------------------------------------

1. 11. °तिलकाभ्युदरणञ्जनं° 38.
2. 12. °होमप्रयोगकथनं° 42*b*.

श्रीं नमः श्रीवज्रसत्याय । श्रीं नमो भगवत्यै आर्यसर्वदुर्गति-  
परिशोधनराजसे (?)

This portion of the work consists entirely of invocations and the like.

दृष्ट्वा वजाभिषिञ्चः । द्रुमवोचङ्गवानात्तमनाः शकब्रह्मा-  
दिदेवा मनुषासुरगन्धर्वयचराचसादिभिसुवाप्ताय (?) भगवतो

Begins, after invocation to Vajrasatva :

एवं मया श्रु० ए० स० भगवान् सर्वदेवोतमनन्दवाने *(sic)*  
विहरति स्म । मनिस्वर्णं....

Ends (98, l. 5) as Add. 1378 (which see); after which follows :

देव-धर्मोऽयं प्रवरमहायानयामिन-परमोपाशकपरमधा-  
र्मिक-धर्मात्मा-वज्राचार्यश्रीचतुरानन्दप्रमुखदि-सुगतानां ॥

Then follow the usual acknowledgment to parents, teachers, etc., and a mention of the reigning king, Bhūpatindra Malla.

After this the scribe gives details as to his family and patrons, and adds some notes in the vernacular. The date, etc., are thus given :

सम्बत् ८२० कार्तिकमास्य *(sic)* शुक्लपक्षे त्रयोदश्यां तिथौ  
रेवतीनक्षत्रे वज्रयोगे यथाकर्णमुहूर्ते वृहस्पतिवासरे तुलेरा-  
सिगते सवितरि मितरासिगते चन्द्रमसि एतत् दिने लिखितं  
सम्पूर्णमिति । लिखितेयं श्री काष्टामण्डप-महानगरे *(sic)* श्री तरु-

214------------------------------------------------

मुर\*-महाविहारे वत्तावाहारनिनयता (?) खण्डचुक गृहातयं  
 असनि (?) श्री ३ वज्रदेव्या चरणाम्बुजयादस्थविक-महाप्राप्तकु-  
 रवज्जीभावाचार्यश्रीरविपतिदेवेन लिखितं संपूर्णमिति ।

The colophon concludes with a scribe's verse.

### Add. 1625.

Paper; 406 leaves, 7 lines,  $14 \times 4\frac{1}{2}$  in.; dated N. S. 948 (A.D. 1828).

The MS. is bound in coloured boards, with designs, etc., on both sides. On leaf 1 is a brightly coloured picture of the deity enthroned and adored by two devotees bearing sacred vessels. Each page is bordered by a rim of red and green stripes.

For the work see Add. 866.

After date, etc., as given above, the scribe proceeds :

°तस्मिन् दिने श्री अष्टसाहस्रिकाप्रज्ञापारमिता पुस्तक पद्मा-  
 वती-महाविहारावस्थित शाक्यवंशीद्भव-श्रीपूर्णजोतिज-तिरिचं-  
 द्रलक्ष्मि-यतियामनसहर्षचित्त उत्पन्तिजयाडी श्रीमहावीद्भवा-  
 श्रीजीवानन्दज नचौचकं प्रतिष्ठापाठादिनजलौ श्रुतम् ।

### Add. 1628.

Paper; 594 leaves, 9 lines, 17 × 4 in.; on the date see below.

PAṄCAVIṂṠATISĀHASRIKĀ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

Leaves 412—559 are paper of the xviii<sup>th</sup> or early xviii<sup>th</sup> cent.; the rest is recent supply. The work is in 8 parivarttas, and is preceded by an introductory treatise in 8 adhikāras, ascribed to Maitreyanātha (leaf 9); see next MS.

Begins :

यः सर्वज्ञतया नयत्युपशमं शान्तौषिणः आवकान् । या मार्ग-  
ज्ञतया जगद्धितक्षता लोकार्थसंपादिका । सर्वाकारमिदं  
वदन्ति मुनयो विश्वं यथा संगतं । तस्मै आवकबोधिसत्त्वगणिनो  
बुद्धस्य मात्रे नमः ॥

The titles of the chapters are given under the next MS., in which they are more clearly marked.

### Add. 1629.

Paper; 384 leaves (and cover), 12 lines, 12½ × 7 in.; dated in words [N. s.] 9..5.

PAṄCAVIṂṠATISĀHASRIKĀ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ.

The word ॥पञ्चविंशति॥ is written on the left margin of 1; there is a large illustration on the same page.

For the beginning see the last MS.

Chapters etc. end as follows :

1. अभिसमयालङ्कारे प्रज्ञापारमितोपदेशशस्त्रे सर्वाकार-  
ज्ञता ऽधिकारः प्रथमः । 2b.

216------------------------------------------------

2. °शास्त्रे मार्गज्ञाताधिकारो द्वितीयः परिच्छेदः। 3.

3. °स्त्रे सर्वज्ञताधिकारसूतीयः परिच्छेदः। 3b.

4. °स्त्रे सर्वकाराभिसंबोधिकारश्चतुर्थः प०। 4b.

The 5th (मूढाभिसमयाधि°), 6th (अनुपूर्वाभिसमयाधि°) and 7th (एकचूणाभि°) adhikāras end on 5b.

°शास्त्रे धर्मकामाधिकारो ऽष्टमः 6.

With these compare the account of the work in Rājendralāl, *Nep. B. L.* 193.

अभिसमयालङ्कारनाम प्रज्ञापारमितोपदेशशास्त्र समाप्तम्। छतिरार्यमैत्रेयनाथस्य [°चार्य° Add. 1628, lf. 9]

The main work begins on the next page (6b) with the usual एवमस्या श्रुतम् and a list of Bodhisattvas, etc.

The first parivartta has no regular subdivision: much of it is in the form of a dialogue between Subhūti and Āriputra. It ends thus:

आर्यपञ्चविंशतिसाहस्रिकायां भगवत्यां प्रज्ञापारमितायामभिसमयालङ्कारानुसारेण स शोधितायां सर्वकारज्ञताधिकारः सुभूतिपरिवर्त्तः प्रथमः। १। 126b.

आर्य° मन्त्रद्वयविशुद्धि प० द्वितीयः। २। 190b.

आर्य° तथतापरिवर्त्तः चतुर्थः। 270b (= 411b, Add. 1623).

आर्य° शिचापरिशुद्धिप० पञ्चमः। 330b (= 503 *ibid.*).

आर्य° अनुक्रमशिचाप० षष्ठः। 334b (= 510 *ibid.*).

आर्य° शिचापरिसमाप्तिप० सप्तमः। 343b.

Ends:

आर्य° शिचाप० अष्टमः।

A number of verses follow, by the scribe Indramuni, both in Sanskrit and the vernacular.

ÇAṬASĀHASRĪ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ, Part 2.

पैति ॥

शतसाह् पारमिं पञ्चविंशं प० । इति द्वितीयखण्डः

A verse or two follows in praise of the work, etc.

#### Add. 1631.

Paper ; 490 leaves, 10 lines,  $20 \times 5\frac{1}{2}$  in. ; dated in words N. S. 983 (A.D. 1863).

ÇAṬASĀHASRĪ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ. Part 3 (Chapters 26—37).

Text begins :

अथ खल्लायुञ्चाच्च (*sic*) शारीपुत्रो भगवन्तमेतद्वोचत् यो  
भगवन्तम् etc. (see Add. 1627).

Ch. 26 ends 252.

Ends :

ॐ अदया ऽद्वैधीकारा ॥ आर्यश्री शतसहस्राः (*sic*) प्रश्नं अष्ट-  
चिंशत्तमः परिव० । Then follows the date (see above) । श्री चन्द्र-  
न्तदेवः रत्नविलासदेवाकुलां विलासदेव-रत्नचूडामणिस्तथा ।  
एते सहानुमतेन स्वस्वहस्तौ लिखिता श्रीमत्प्रज्ञापारमिता चय-  
षत (?) संपूर्णैति ॥

#### Add. 1632.

Paper ; 607 leaves, 10—11 lines,  $17 \times 5$  in. ; dated N. S. 923 (A.D. 1803).

ÇAṬASĀHASRĪ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ. Part 4 (Chapters 38—72).

218------------------------------------------------

Text begins :

प्रज्ञापारमितामेव प्राप्तां प्रज्ञाश्रयोदयां। प्रज्ञाप्रख्यातसत्की-  
र्तितां प्रज्ञां प्रणमास्यहं॥ पितरं बोधिसत्त्वानां सुगतं गत-  
किल्विषं। प्रज्ञाप्राप्तनिर्वाणं नमामि कर्तृणात्मकं॥

Parivartta 38 ends 60b; 39, 72b; 40, 78b; 41, 96b; 50, 184;  
55, 509; 60, 590b.

Ends, 607 :

शतसाहस्र्याः प्रज्ञापारमिताया अकीर्णधर्मतानिर्देश द्वा-  
सप्ततितमः परिवर्तः। (This is the only instance I have observed  
in this MS. of the indication of a chapter's contents at the end.)  
इदमवोचद्गवान्<sup>०</sup> etc. .... भाषितमभ्यनन्दन्ति । समाप्ता  
चैयमार्यप्रज्ञापारमिता शतसाहस्रिका दशदिगनन्तपर्यन्तली-  
कधातुसमुद्रपर्यापन्नसर्वबुद्ध<sup>०</sup> ..... प्रत्युत्पन्नानां माता<sup>०</sup> धर्म-  
समुद्रा... अभिधर्मरत्ननिधाना... and so on, in praise of the  
book and its study, for a dozen lines; after which date and name of  
scribe, as follows :

याते नैपालिकवर्षे त्रियुग्मरन्ध्रसंयुते। मार्गशीर्षे ऽश्विनपक्षे  
दशम्यां रविवाररे शतसाहस्रिकाग्रं रत्नानन्देन लिख्यते।

The place of writing (Lalitā-pura) and the reigning monarch  
(Gīrvāṇa-yuddha) are also mentioned.

In spite of the mention of a fifth Khaṇḍa in Dr D. Wright's list,  
which however does not appear in our collection, it would seem from  
the tone and form of the above colophon, that this is either the  
conclusion of the whole work, or that at all events the account given  
by Mr Hodgson's Paṇḍit (Hodgs. Essay Lit. Nep. p. 16) is correct;  
that "Satā Sāhasrika is a collective name of the first four Khands, to  
which the fifth is not necessarily adjunct; and indeed it is one of the  
several abstracts of the Sāhasrika." The Tibetan version (see Csoma,  
and Feer in Ann. G. ii. 197) has 75 chapters.

### Add. 1633.

Palm-leaf; 494 leaves, 11 lines, 17½ × 5 in.; dated N. s. 926  
(A.D. 1806).

219------------------------------------------------

ĀTASĀHASRĪ PRAJÑĀPĀRAMITĀ, Part 1.

There is an illustration on leaf 1.

“प्रथम” is written on the left margin of many leaves.

The MS. is enclosed by a pair of very finely carved boards.

This appears to be the second known redaction of the Prajñāpāramitā, the chief philosophical work of Northern Buddhism.

It abounds in repetitions, etc., in the most wearisome style of Buddhist literature. For general notices see Hodgson Ess. 16, Burn. Intr. 462 etc., and especially Rājendralāl, *Nep. Buddh. Lit.* pp. 177, sqq.

Notices of the Tibetan versions are given in Wassiliew and Csoma (Feer in Ann. Guimet, ii. 197).

Begins :

एवं मया श्रु० ए० स० भ० राजगृहे वि० स०। गृध्रकूटपर्वते  
महता भिचुसंधेन सार्द्धं पञ्चमात्रैर्भिचुसहस्रैः०

Parivartta i. ends 96 ; ii. 139 ; iii. 147b ; iv. ? ; v. 265b ; vi. 354 ;  
vii. 416 ; viii. 419 ; ix. 426b ; x. 450b ; xi. 482.

Ends :

आर्यशतसाहस्र्या भगवत्याः प्रज्ञापारमितायाः प्रथमखण्डं  
समाप्तमिति ॥ ये धर्मा० । गुरुभं etc. । समत् ५२७ वैशाषमास्य  
गुरुकूपचे चतुर्दश्यांतिथौ चिन्तानचत्रे वजयोगे यथाकर्ममही-  
न्तक (?) – बृहस्पति – वार – शकुह – श्रीरचभगवतिः प्रथमख-  
ण्डप्रज्ञापारमितापुस्तक चौपधुनकादिनजरो । ० । लिखित श्री-  
मानदेवसंस्कारितचक्रमहाविहाराधिष्ठितः श्रीवज्राचार्य ज्ञा-  
जणग्लन (?) संपूर्णमिति ।

Then follows a line or two as to the writing, and in praise of the book.

Add. 1634.

Paper; 37 leaves, 5 lines (ruled in red),  $7\frac{1}{2} \times 2$  in.; dated in words N. s. 772 (A.D. 1652).

KĪRTIPATĀKĀ by KUNÜÇARMAN.

In 126 numbered verses.

220------------------------------------------------

Begins :

यं भक्त्या हरिधाहशक्रविवृधा देवारयश्चानिश्  
 यत्यादाञ्जनिषेवनादपगतकूरातिविघ्नारयः ।  
 आराध्यार्णवमन्यने हरमुखात् प्राप्नुन्ति\* सिद्धिं शिवं  
 सो ऽयं वो हि गजाननश्च दुरितं दूरीकरोतु प्रभुः ॥  
 केचिज्जनैर् [? कैश्चि?] गृहस्थानं पृष्टं पुर्याश्च मां प्रति ।  
 श्रीकुनूनामधेयो ऽहं तदर्थनं करोम्यतः ॥  
 प्रासादमठदेवानां प्रणाली कूपसन्ननां ।  
 नेपालनामधेयैश्च रथामण्डपवर्त्तनां ॥

Then follows another introductory verse.

The work thus appears to be a description of certain places in Nepal. It commences with Lalitapura thus :

अस्ति श्रीललिताभिधाननगरी नेपालभूमण्डले ।

Ends :

इति श्रीकुनूशर्मेना विरचिता कीर्तिपताका समाप्ता । नयन-  
 मुनिसगैलेरङ्गितातीतसङ्ख्ये etc. (see above).
